<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>
* If there are existing git user credentials already configured, use them and add Co-authored-by: openhands <openhands@all-hands.dev> to any commits messages you make. if a git config doesn't exist use "openhands" as the user.name and "openhands@all-hands.dev" as the user.email by default, unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* Exercise caution with git operations. Do NOT make potentially dangerous changes (e.g., pushing to main, deleting repositories) unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When committing changes, use `git status` to see all modified files, and stage all files necessary for the commit. Use `git commit -a` whenever possible.
* Do NOT commit files that typically shouldn't go into version control (e.g., node_modules/, .env files, build directories, cache files, large binaries) unless explicitly instructed by the user.
* If unsure about committing certain files, check for the presence of .gitignore files or ask the user for clarification.
</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PULL_REQUESTS>
* **Important**: Do not push to the remote branch and/or start a pull request unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When creating pull requests, create only ONE per session/issue unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* When working with an existing PR, update it with new commits rather than creating additional PRs for the same issue.
* When updating a PR, preserve the original PR title and purpose, updating description only when necessary.
</PULL_REQUESTS>

<PROBLEM_SOLVING_WORKFLOW>
1. EXPLORATION: Thoroughly explore relevant files and understand the context before proposing solutions
2. ANALYSIS: Consider multiple approaches and select the most promising one
3. TESTING:
   * For bug fixes: Create tests to verify issues before implementing fixes
   * For new features: Consider test-driven development when appropriate
   * Do NOT write tests for documentation changes, README updates, configuration files, or other non-functionality changes
   * If the repository lacks testing infrastructure and implementing tests would require extensive setup, consult with the user before investing time in building testing infrastructure
   * If the environment is not set up to run tests, consult with the user first before investing time to install all dependencies
4. IMPLEMENTATION:
   * Make focused, minimal changes to address the problem
   * Always modify existing files directly rather than creating new versions with different suffixes
   * If you create temporary files for testing, delete them after confirming your solution works
5. VERIFICATION: If the environment is set up to run tests, test your implementation thoroughly, including edge cases. If the environment is not set up to run tests, consult with the user first before investing time to run tests.
</PROBLEM_SOLVING_WORKFLOW>

<SECURITY>
* Only use GITHUB_TOKEN and other credentials in ways the user has explicitly requested and would expect.
* Use APIs to work with GitHub or other platforms, unless the user asks otherwise or your task requires browsing.
</SECURITY>

<EXTERNAL_SERVICES>
* When interacting with external services like GitHub, GitLab, or Bitbucket, use their respective APIs instead of browser-based interactions whenever possible.
* Only resort to browser-based interactions with these services if specifically requested by the user or if the required operation cannot be performed via API.
</EXTERNAL_SERVICES>

<ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>
* When user asks you to run an application, don't stop if the application is not installed. Instead, please install the application and run the command again.
* If you encounter missing dependencies:
  1. First, look around in the repository for existing dependency files (requirements.txt, pyproject.toml, package.json, Gemfile, etc.)
  2. If dependency files exist, use them to install all dependencies at once (e.g., `pip install -r requirements.txt`, `npm install`, etc.)
  3. Only install individual packages directly if no dependency files are found or if only specific packages are needed
* Similarly, if you encounter missing dependencies for essential tools requested by the user, install them when possible.
</ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>

<TROUBLESHOOTING>
* If you've made repeated attempts to solve a problem but tests still fail or the user reports it's still broken:
  1. Step back and reflect on 5-7 different possible sources of the problem
  2. Assess the likelihood of each possible cause
  3. Methodically address the most likely causes, starting with the highest probability
  4. Document your reasoning process
* When you run into any major issue while executing a plan from the user, please don't try to directly work around it. Instead, propose a new plan and confirm with the user before proceeding.
</TROUBLESHOOTING>

<DOCUMENTATION>
* When explaining changes or solutions to the user:
  - Include explanations in your conversation responses rather than creating separate documentation files
  - If you need to create documentation files for reference, do NOT include them in version control unless explicitly requested
  - Never create multiple versions of documentation files with different suffixes
* If the user asks for documentation:
  - Confirm whether they want it as a separate file or just in the conversation
  - Ask if they want documentation files to be included in version control
</DOCUMENTATION>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

<TASK_MANAGEMENT>
* You have access to the `task_tracker` tool to help you organize and monitor development work. Use this tool REGULARLY to maintain task visibility and provide users with clear progress updates. This tool is ESSENTIAL for systematic planning and decomposing complex development work into manageable components. Failing to use this tool for planning may result in overlooked requirements - which is unacceptable.
* It is crucial that you update task status to "done" immediately upon completion of each work item. Do not accumulate multiple finished tasks before updating their status.
* For complex, multi-phase development work, use `task_tracker` to establish a comprehensive plan with well-defined steps:
  1. Begin by decomposing the overall objective into primary phases using `task_tracker`
  2. Include detailed work items as necessary to break complex activities into actionable units
  3. Update tasks to "in_progress" status when commencing work on them
  4. Update tasks to "done" status immediately after completing each item
  5. For each primary phase, incorporate additional work items as you identify new requirements
  6. If you determine the plan requires substantial modifications, suggest revisions and obtain user confirmation before proceeding
* Example workflow for debugging and resolution:

     1	# Type-Length-Value8 (TLV8) for python [![Build Status](https://travis-ci.org/jlusiardi/tlv8_python.svg?branch=master)](https://travis-ci.org/jlusiardi/tlv8_python) [![Coverage Status](https://coveralls.io/repos/github/jlusiardi/tlv8_python/badge.svg?branch=master)](https://coveralls.io/github/jlusiardi/tlv8_python?branch=master) 
     2	
     3	Type-Length-Value (TLV) are used to encode arbitrary data. In this case the type and length are represented by 1 byte each. Hence the name TLV8.
     4	
     5	A TLV8 entry consists of the following parts:
     6	
     7	 * the **type**: this 8 bit field denotes the type of information that is represented by the data.
     8	 * the **length**: this 8 bit field denotes the length of the data (this does not include the 2 bytes for type and length. For data longer than 255 bytes, there is a defined procedure available.
     9	 * the **value**: these **length** bytes represent the value of this TLV. The different types of data is represented differently:
    10	   * **bytes**: this is raw binary data and will be used as is, no further interpretation takes place
    11	   * **tlv8**: this is a specialized case of  **bytes** values. Using this instead of pure bytes enables nesting of data and creating a hierarchy.
    12	   * **integer**: integers are stored in little-endian byte order and are encoded with the minimal number of bytes possible (1, 2, 4 or 8)
    13	   * **float**: floats are stored as little-endian ieee754 numbers
    14	   * **string**: strings are always UTF-8 encoded and **do not** contain the terminating NULL byte
    15	
    16	TLV8 entries whose content is longer than 255 bytes are split up into fragments. The type is repeated is repeated in each fragment, only the last fragment may contain less than 255 bytes. Fragments of one TLV8 entry must be continuous.
    17	
    18	Multiple TLV8 entries can be combined to create larger structures. Entries of different types can placed one after another. Entries of the same type must be separated by a TLV8 entry of a different type (and probably zero length).
    19	
    20	TLV8 entries of unknown or unwanted type are to be silently ignored.
    21	
    22	## Examples 
    23	
    24	### simple TLV8s
    25	
    26	Encoding of some atomic examples:
    27	
    28	 * an empty TLV of type 42: `[42, None]` will be encoded as `b'\x2a\x00'`.
    29	 * a TLV of type 2 with 2 bytes `0x12, 0x34`: `[2, b'\x12\x34']` will be encoded as `b'\x02\x02\x12\x34'`
    30	 * a TLV of type 3 that contains the TLV from above: `[3, [2, b'\x12\x34']]` will be encoded as `b'\x03\x04\x02\x02\x12\x34'`
    31	 * a TLV of type 4 that contains 1024: `[4, 1024]` will be encoded as `b'\x04\0x02\x00\x04'`
    32	 * a TLV of type 5 that contains 3.141: `[4, 3.141]` will be encoded as `b'\x04\x04\x0a\xd7\x23\x41'`
    33	 * a TLV of type 23 with string `Hello 🌍`: `[23, 'Hello 🌍']` will be encoded as `b'\x17\x0a\x48\x65\x6c\x6c\x6f\x20\xf0\x9f\x8c\x8d'`
    34	
    35	### fragmented TLV8s
    36	
    37	Encoding of a fragmented TLV8 entry:
    38	
    39	 * an TLV of type 6 that contains 256 bytes from 0 to 255: `[6, b'\x00\x01...\xfe\xff']` will be encoded as `b'\x06\xff\x00...\xfe\x06\x01\xff'`
    40	
    41	### combined TLV8s
    42	
    43	Encoding of two TLV8 Entries that follow each other in the input list:
    44	
    45	 * the combination of 2 TLV8 entries (`[1, 123]` and `[2, 'Hello']`) will be encoded as `b'\x01\x01\x7b\x02\x05\x48\x65\x6c\x6c\x6f'`
    46	
    47	### sequences of TLV8s of same type:
    48	
    49	 * a sequence of 3 TLV8 entries of type 1 (`[1, 1]`, `[1, 2]` and `[1, 1]`) will be encoded as `b'\x01\x01\x01\xff\x00\x01\x01\x02\xff\x00\x01\x01\x03'`
    50	
    51	## Using in code
    52	
    53	There are two main use cases of this module.
    54	
    55	### Create a bytes representation
    56	
    57	Here we want to have a comfortable way to create a data structure in python and to encode this structure into a bytes value.
    58	
    59	#### encode a simple list
    60	For example, create a representation containing the following structure:
    61	 
    62	 * Type: 1, Value: 23
    63	 * Type: 2, Value: 2345
    64	
    65	This can be code like that:
    66	
    67	```python
    68	import tlv8
    69	
    70	structure = [
    71	    tlv8.Entry(1, 23),
    72	    tlv8.Entry(2, 2345)
    73	]
    74	bytes_data = tlv8.encode(structure)
    75	print(bytes_data)
    76	```
    77	
    78	And this will result in: `b'\x01\x01\x17\x02\x02)\t'`
    79	
    80	#### Nesting structures
    81	
    82	Representing a line ([x: 10, y: 20] - [x: 30, y: 40]) between to points could be represented like:
    83	
    84	 * Type: 1, Value:
    85	   * Type: 3, Value: 10 
    86	   * Type: 4, Value: 20
    87	 * Type: 2, Value:
    88	   * Type: 3, Value: 30
    89	   * Type: 4, Value: 40
    90	
    91	```python
    92	import tlv8
    93	
    94	structure = [
    95	    tlv8.Entry(1, [
    96	        tlv8.Entry(3, 10),
    97	        tlv8.Entry(4, 10),
    98	    ]),
    99	    tlv8.Entry(2, [
   100	        tlv8.Entry(3, 30),
   101	        tlv8.Entry(4, 40),
   102	    ])
   103	]
   104	bytes_data = tlv8.encode(structure)
   105	print(bytes_data)
   106	```
   107	
   108	And this will result in: `b'\x01\x06\x03\x01\n\x04\x01\n\x02\x06\x03\x01\x1e\x04\x01('`
   109	
   110	### Decode a bytes representation
   111	
   112	Decoding TLV8 entries from bytes data will return all bytes from all first level entries. This includes possible separator entries between entries of the same type.
   113	
   114	Decoding can be assisted by hinting with an expected structure. To represent the structure in python `dict` objects are used and nested. The keys of the `dict` objects are the type ids of the TLV8 entries. If the id of an entry is not contained in the structure, it will be ignored.
   115	
   116	#### decode the simple list
   117	
   118	```python
   119	import tlv8
   120	
   121	in_data = b'\x01\x01\x17\x02\x02)\t'
   122	expected_structure = {
   123	    1: tlv8.DataType.INTEGER,
   124	    2: tlv8.DataType.INTEGER
   125	}
   126	result = tlv8.decode(in_data, expected_structure)
   127	
   128	print(tlv8.format_string(result))
   129	```
   130	
   131	This will result in:
   132	```text
   133	[
   134	  <1, 23>,
   135	  <2, 2345>,
   136	]
   137	```
   138	
   139	#### decode nested data
   140	
   141	```python
   142	import tlv8
   143	
   144	in_data = b'\x01\x06\x03\x01\n\x04\x01\n\x02\x06\x03\x01\x1e\x04\x01('
   145	sub_struct = {
   146	    3: tlv8.DataType.INTEGER,
   147	    4: tlv8.DataType.INTEGER
   148	}
   149	expected_structure = {
   150	    1: sub_struct,
   151	    2: sub_struct
   152	}
   153	result = tlv8.decode(in_data, expected_structure)
   154	
   155	print(tlv8.format_string(result))
   156	```
   157	
   158	This will result in:
   159	```text
   160	[
   161	  <1, [
   162	    <3, 10>,
   163	    <4, 10>,
   164	  ]>,
   165	  <2, [
   166	    <3, 30>,
   167	    <4, 40>,
   168	  ]>,
   169	]
   170	```
   171	
   172	### Using IntEnum data during encoding and decoding
   173	
   174	Using enumerations might increase readabilty of encode end decode processes.
   175	
   176	### During encoding
   177	
   178	It is possible to use `enum.IntEnum` for encoding:
   179	
   180	```python
   181	import tlv8
   182	import enum
   183	
   184	class Keys(enum.IntEnum):
   185	    X = 42
   186	    # ...
   187	
   188	class Values(enum.IntEnum):
   189	    Y = 23
   190	    # ...
   191	
   192	result = tlv8.encode([
   193	    tlv8.Entry(Keys.X, Values.Y)
   194	])
   195	
   196	print(result)
   197	```
   198	
   199	This will result in:
   200	```text
   201	b'*\x01\x17'
   202	```
   203	
   204	### During decoding
   205	
   206	As during encoding, `enum.IntEnum` can be used for keys and values during decoding:
   207	
   208	```python
   209	import tlv8
   210	import enum
   211	
   212	class Keys(enum.IntEnum):
   213	    X = 42
   214	    # ...
   215	
   216	class Values(enum.IntEnum):
   217	    Y = 23
   218	    # ...
   219	
   220	result = tlv8.decode(b'*\x01\x17', {
   221	    Keys.X: Values
   222	})
   223	
   224	print(tlv8.format_string(result))
   225	print(type(result[0].type_id), type(result[0].data))
   226	```
   227	
   228	This will result in 
   229	```text
   230	[
   231	  <Keys.X, Values.Y>,
   232	]
   233	<enum 'Keys'> <enum 'Values'>
   234	```
   235	
   236	So the `type_id` and the `data` fields are not simple `int` instance anymore but values of their enumerations. This alos helps during using `format_string` to get a easier to read output.
   237	
   238	## Coding
   239	
   240	The module offers the following primary functions and classes.
   241	
   242	### function `format_string`
   243	
   244	This function formats a list of TLV8 Entry objects as str. The hierarchy of the entries will be represented by increasing the indentation of the output.
   245	
   246	The parameters are:
   247	 
   248	 * `entries`: a python list of tlv8.Entries objects
   249	 * `indent`: the level of indentation to be used, this defaults to 0 and is increased on recursive calls for nested entries.
   250	 
   251	The function returns a `str` instance and raises `ValueError` instances if the input is not a list of `tlv8.Entry` objects.
   252	
   253	Example:
   254	```python
   255	import tlv8
   256	
   257	data = [
   258	    tlv8.Entry(1, 3.141),
   259	    tlv8.Entry(2, [
   260	        tlv8.Entry(3, 'hello'),
   261	        tlv8.Entry(4, 'world'),
   262	    ]),
   263	    tlv8.Entry(1, 2)
   264	]
   265	print(tlv8.format_string(data))
   266	```
   267	
   268	This will become:
   269	```text
   270	[
   271	  <1, 3.141>,
   272	  <2, [
   273	    <3, hello>,
   274	    <4, world>,
   275	  ]>,
   276	  <1, 2>,
   277	]
   278	```
   279	
   280	### function `encode`
   281	
   282	Function to encode a list of `tlv8.Entry` objects into a sequence of bytes following the rules for creating TLVs. The `separator_type_id` is used for the separating entries between two entries of the same type. 
   283	
   284	The parameters are:
   285	
   286	 * `entries`: a list of `tlv8.Entry` objects
   287	 * `separator_type_id`: the 8-bit type id of the separator to be used. The default is (as defined in table 5-6, page 51 of HomeKit Accessory Protocol Specification Non-Commercial Version Release R2) 0xff.
   288	
   289	The function returns an instance of `bytes`. This is empty if nothing was encoded. The function raises `ValueError` if the input parameter is not a list of `tlv8.Entry` objects or a data value is not encodable. A `ValueError` will also be raised if the `separator_type_id` is used as `type_id` in one of the entries as well.
   290	
   291	Example:
   292	```python
   293	import tlv8
   294	
   295	data = [
   296	    tlv8.Entry(1, 3.141),
   297	    tlv8.Entry(2, [
   298	        tlv8.Entry(3, 'hello'),
   299	        tlv8.Entry(4, 'world')
   300	    ]),
   301	    tlv8.Entry(1, 2)
   302	]
   303	print(tlv8.encode(data))
   304	```
   305	
   306	This will result in:
   307	```text
   308	b'\x01\x04%\x06I@\x02\x0e\x03\x05hello\x04\x05world\x01\x01\x02'
   309	```
   310	
   311	### function `decode`
   312	
   313	Function to decode a `bytes` or `bytearray` instance into a list of `tlv8.Entry` instances. This reverses the process done by the `encode` function.
   314	
   315	The parameters are:
   316	
   317	 * `data`: a `bytes` or `bytearray` instance to be parsed
   318	 * `expected`: a dict of type ids onto expected `tlv8.DataType` values. If the expected entry is again a `tlv8.Entry` that should be parsed, use another dict to describe the hiearchical structure. This defaults to `None` which means not filtering will be performed but also no interpretation of the entries is done. This means they will be returned as `bytes` sequence.
   319	 * `strict_mode`: This defaults to `False`. If set to `True`, this will raise additional `ValueError` instances if there are possible missing separators between entries of the same type.
   320	
   321	The function returns a `list` instance and raises `ValueError` instances if the input is either not a `bytes` object or an invalid tlv8 structure.
   322	
   323	Example:
   324	```python
   325	import tlv8
   326	
   327	data = b'\x01\x04%\x06I@\x02\x0e\x03\x05hello\x04\x05world\x03\x01\x02'
   328	
   329	structure = {
   330	        1: tlv8.DataType.FLOAT,
   331	        2: {
   332	            3: tlv8.DataType.STRING,
   333	            4: tlv8.DataType.STRING
   334	        },
   335	        3: tlv8.DataType.INTEGER
   336	    }
   337	
   338	print(tlv8.decode(data, structure))
   339	```
   340	
   341	This will result in:
   342	```text
   343	[
   344	  <1, 3.1410000324249268>,
   345	  <2, [
   346	    <3, hello>,
   347	    <4, world>,
   348	  ]>,
   349	  <3, 2>,
   350	]
   351	```
   352	
   353	### function `deep_decode`
   354	
   355	This function works like the `decode` function but tries to do it recursively. That means it decodes the first level of
   356	a TLV8 structure first, then looks at each entry and tries to decode that as well. This is mostly meant for debugging
   357	purposes in combination with `format_string`.
   358	
   359	Example:
   360	```python
   361	import tlv8
   362	
   363	data = b'\x01\x01\x23\x02\x03\x04\x01\x42\x01\x01\x23'
   364	print(tlv8.deep_decode(data))
   365	
   366	```
   367	
   368	This will result in:
   369	```text
   370	[
   371	  <1, b'#'>,
   372	  <2, [
   373	    <4, b'B'>,
   374	  ]>,
   375	  <1, b'#'>,
   376	]
   377	
   378	```
   379	
   380	**Notice**:
   381	
   382	This function might misinterpret data as TLV8 data. For example
   383	
   384	```python
   385	import tlv8
   386	
   387	data = tlv8.encode([
   388	    tlv8.Entry(1, 16843330),
   389	    tlv8.Entry(2, b'\x01')
   390	])
   391	
   392	# here data is b'\x01\x04B\x02\x01\x01\x02\x01\x01'
   393	
   394	print(tlv8.deep_decode(data))
   395	```
   396	
   397	This will result in a misinterpretation of the entry with ID 1:
   398	```text
   399	[
   400	  <1, [
   401	    <66, b'\x01\x01'>,
   402	  ]>,
   403	  <2, b'\x01'>,
   404	]
   405	```
   406	
   407	
   408	
   409	### class `DataType`
   410	
   411	This enumeration is used to represent the data type of a `tlv8.Entry`. 
   412	
   413	Enumeration Entry | TLV8 type | Python type
   414	---               | ---       | ---
   415	BYTES             | bytes     | `bytes`, also `bytearray` for encoding
   416	TLV8              | tlv8      | custom class `tlv8.Entry` for encoding and `dict` for the expected structure during decoding
   417	INTEGER           | integer   | `int`
   418	FLOAT             | float     | `float`
   419	STRING            | string    | `str`
   420	AUTODETECT        | n/a       | this is used declare that a data type is not preset but will be determined by the python type of the data
   421	
   422	
   423	### class `Entry`
   424	
   425	This class represents a single entry in a TLV8 data set. The class overrides the methods `__eq__`, `__str__` and `__repr__` to fit the needs of the application.
   426	
   427	#### constructor
   428	
   429	The constructor takes the following parameters:
   430	
   431	 * `type_id`: the type id of the entry. Must be between 0 and 255 (8-bit type id).
   432	 * `data`: the data to be stored in this entry. 
   433	 * `data_type`: the data type of the entry. Defaults to `DataType.AUTODETECT`.
   434	 * `length`: if set, this overrides the automatic length detection. This used for integer, when there is special need to set higher byte count than the value would need.
   435	 
   436	The constructor raises a `ValueError` if the `type_id` is not within the 8-bit range.
   437	
   438	#### `encode() -> bytes`
   439	
   440	This function is called to encode the data stored in this `Entry`. The data type of the data will be used to decide how to encode the data. It uses the `tlv8.encode()` function to encode nested lists of `tlv8.Entry` objects. 
   441	
   442	#### `format_string() -> str`
   443	
   444	This function formats the data stored in this entry as readable string. It is mostly called by `tlv8.format_string()`.
   445	
   446	### class `EntryList`
   447	
   448	This class represents a list of entries. The class overrides the methods `__repr__`, `__eq__`, `__len__`, `__getitem__` and `__iter__` to fit the needs of the application. 
   449	
   450	#### constructor
   451	
   452	The constructor takes the following parameters:
   453	
   454	 * `data`: if set, this `list` of `tlv8.Entry` instances is used to initialize the `EntryList`.
   455	
   456	The constructor raised a `ValueError` if the data is either not a `list` or not a list of `tlv8.Entry` instances.
   457	
   458	#### `append(entry)`
   459	
   460	Append the `tlv8.Entry` to the `EntryList`. It performs type checks, so only `tlv8.Entry` instances can be appended.
   461	
   462	#### `assert_has(type_id, message)`
   463	
   464	Looks for a `tlv8.Entry` instance with `type_id` in the first level of the `EntryList`. If none is found, it raises an `AssertionError` with the given `message`. This does not iterate recursivly, because the same type id may have different meanings on different levels (and different contexts).
   465	
   466	#### `encode(self, separator_type_id)`
   467	
   468	Encodes the `EntryList` using the given separator type id. This relies on `tlv8.encode()`.
   469	
   470	#### `by_id(type_id)`
   471	
   472	Filters the `EntryList` and returns only `Entry` instance whose `type_id` match the given one. If no `Entry` instances
   473	were found it returns an empty list.
   474	
   475	#### `first_by_id(type_id)`
   476	
   477	Search the `EntryList` for the first `Entry` with the given `type_id`. If no such `Entry` was found, it returns `None`.
   478

    28	    def isclose(a, b, rel_tol=1e-09, abs_tol=0.0):
    29	        return abs(a - b) <= max(rel_tol * max(abs(a), abs(b)), abs_tol)

    32	class EntryList(object):
    33	    def __init__(self, data=None):
    34	        """
    35	        Create a new EntryList instance. It is initialized with the given data
    36	
    37	        :param data: this must be a list of Entry instances
    38	        :raises: ValueError is risen if either data is not a list or not all list entries are Entry instances
    39	        """
    40	        if data:
    41	            if isinstance(data, list):
    42	                for entry in data:
    43	                    if not isinstance(entry, Entry):
    44	                        raise ValueError('Not a valid tlv8.Entry: {e}'.format(e=entry))
    45	                self.data = data
    46	            else:
    47	                raise ValueError('No valid list: {e}'.format(e=data))
    48	        else:
    49	            self.data = []
    50	
    51	    def append(self, entry):
    52	        """
    53	        Appends an tlv8.Entry to this tlv8.EntryList. This works like list.append().
    54	
    55	        :param entry: the entry to append
    56	        :raises: ValueError if the entry to add is no tlv8.Entry
    57	        """
    58	        if not isinstance(entry, Entry):
    59	            raise ValueError('Not an tlv8.Entry: {e}'.format(e=entry))
    60	        self.data.append(entry)
    61	
    62	    def __iter__(self):
    63	        return self.data.__iter__()
    64	
    65	    def __getitem__(self, item):
    66	        return self.data[item]
    67	
    68	    def __len__(self):
    69	        return self.data.__len__()
    70	
    71	    def assert_has(self, type_id, message=''):
    72	        """
    73	        Scan this entry list for entries with the given type id. If no entry was found an AssertionError with the given
    74	        message is raised.
    75	
    76	        :param type_id: the type id to check for
    77	        :param message: the message of the AssertionError if raised
    78	        :raises: AssertionError if no entry with the type id was found
    79	        """
    80	        if self.first_by_id(type_id):
    81	            return
    82	        raise AssertionError(message)
    83	
    84	    def __eq__(self, other):
    85	        if isinstance(other, self.__class__):
    86	            return self.data == other.data
    87	        else:
    88	            return False
    89	
    90	    def __repr__(self):
    91	        return '<EntryList ' + self.data.__repr__() + '>'
    92	
    93	    def encode(self, separator_type_id=0xff):
    94	        """
    95	        Function to encode this EntryList into a sequence of bytes following the rules for creating TLVs.
    96	
    97	        :param separator_type_id: the 8-bit id of the separator to be used in two fields of the same type id are
    98	            directly after one another in the list. The default is (as defined in table 5-6, page 51 of HomeKit
    99	            Accessory Protocol Specification Non-Commercial Version Release R2) 0xff.
   100	        :return: an instance of bytes. if nothing was encoded, it returns an empty instance
   101	        :raises ValueError: if the input parameter is not conform to a list of tlv8.Entry objects
   102	        """
   103	        return encode(self.data, separator_type_id)
   104	
   105	    def by_id(self, type_id):
   106	        """
   107	        Filters the entry list and returns only those entries whose type is of the given value.
   108	
   109	        :param type_id: the type id to look for
   110	        :return: a EntryList instance containing all found entries, the list may be empty.
   111	        """
   112	        return EntryList([entry for entry in self.data if entry.type_id == type_id])
   113	
   114	    def first_by_id(self, type_id):
   115	        """
   116	        Scan this entry list and return the first entry whose type is of the given value.
   117	
   118	        :param type_id: the type id to look for
   119	        :return: a Entry instance or None, if not a single entry has the searched type id
   120	        """
   121	        for entry in self.data:
   122	            if entry.type_id == type_id:
   123	                return entry
   124	        return None

   126	
   127	def format_string(entries: list, indent=0) -> str:
   128	    """
   129	    Format a list of TLV8 Entry objects or a EntryList as str instance. The hierarchy of the entries will be
   130	    represented by increasing the indentation of the output.
   131	
   132	    Example:
   133	    ```
   134	        data = [
   135	            tlv8.Entry(1, 3.141),
   136	            tlv8.Entry(2, [
   137	                tlv8.Entry(3, 'hello'),
   138	                tlv8.Entry(4, 'world'),
   139	            ]),
   140	            tlv8.Entry(1, 2)
   141	        ]
   142	        print(tlv8.format_string(data))
   143	    ```
   144	    will become:
   145	    ```
   146	        [
   147	          <1, 3.141>,
   148	          <2, [
   149	            <3, hello>,
   150	            <4, world>,
   151	          ]>,
   152	          <1, 2>,
   153	        ]
   154	    ```
   155	
   156	    :param entries: a list of tlv8.Entries objects
   157	    :param indent: the level of indentation to be used
   158	    :return: a str instance with the formatted representation of the input
   159	    :raises ValueError: if the input parameter is not conform to a list of tlv8.Entry objects
   160	    """
   161	    if not (isinstance(entries, list) or isinstance(entries, EntryList)):
   162	        raise ValueError('The parameter entries must be of type list or EntryList')
   163	    result = '[\n'
   164	    for entry in entries:
   165	        if not isinstance(entry, Entry):
   166	            raise ValueError('The parameter entries must only contain elements of type tlv8.Entry')
   167	        result += ' ' * (indent + 2) + entry.format_string(indent + 2) + '\n'
   168	    result += ' ' * indent + ']'
   169	    return result
   170	
   171	
   172	def encode(entries: list, separator_type_id=0xff) -> bytes:
   173	    """
   174	    Function to encode a list of TLV Entry objects into a sequence of bytes following the rules for creating TLVs.
   175	
   176	    :param entries: a list of tlv8.Entries objects
   177	    :param separator_type_id: the 8-bit id of the separator to be used in two fields of the same type id are directly
   178	        after one another in the list. The default is (as defined in table 5-6, page 51 of HomeKit Accessory Protocol
   179	        Specification Non-Commercial Version Release R2) 0xff.
   180	    :return: an instance of bytes. if nothing was encoded, it returns an empty instance
   181	    :raises ValueError: if the input parameter is not conform to a list of tlv8.Entry objects
   182	    """
   183	    if not isinstance(entries, list) and not isinstance(entries, EntryList):
   184	        raise ValueError('The parameter entries must be of type list')
   185	    result = b''
   186	    last_type_id = None
   187	    for entry in entries:
   188	        if not isinstance(entry, Entry):
   189	            raise ValueError('The parameter entries must only contain elements of type tlv8.Entry')
   190	        if entry.type_id == separator_type_id:
   191	            raise ValueError('Separator type id {st} occurs with list of entries!'.format(st=separator_type_id))
   192	        if last_type_id == entry.type_id:
   193	            # must insert separator of two entries of the same type succeed one an other
   194	            result += pack('<B', separator_type_id) + b'\x00'
   195	        result += entry.encode()
   196	        last_type_id = entry.type_id
   197	    return result
   198	
   199	
   200	def _internal_decode(data, strict_mode=False) -> EntryList:
   201	    if isinstance(data, bytearray):
   202	        data = bytes(data)
   203	    if not isinstance(data, bytes):
   204	        raise ValueError('data parameter must be bytes or bytearray not {}'.format(type(data)))
   205	    if len(data) == 0:
   206	        # no data, nothing to do
   207	        return EntryList()
   208	    tmp = EntryList()
   209	    remaining_data = data
   210	    while len(remaining_data) > 0:
   211	        if len(remaining_data) < 2:
   212	            # the shortest encoded TLV8 is 3 bytes, we got less, so raise an error
   213	            raise ValueError('Bytes with length {len} is not a valid TLV8.'.format(len=len(data)))
   214	
   215	        tlv_id = unpack('<B', remaining_data[0:1])[0]
   216	        tlv_len = unpack('<B', remaining_data[1:2])[0]
   217	        if len(remaining_data[2:]) < tlv_len:
   218	            # the remaining data is less than the encoded length
   219	            raise ValueError('Not enough data left. {} vs {}'.format(len(remaining_data[2:]), tlv_len))
   220	        tlv_data = remaining_data[2:2 + tlv_len]
   221	        if len(tmp) > 0 and tmp[-1].type_id == tlv_id:
   222	            # we have the same type id so we expect the size of the data so far to be 0 mod 255
   223	            if len(tmp[-1].data) % 255 != 0:
   224	                # it there was no max size fragment before, this is either
   225	                if strict_mode:
   226	                    # an error in strict mode
   227	                    raise ValueError('Missing separator detected.')
   228	                else:
   229	                    # or we let it pass as a second instance of the type id. both could be wrong
   230	                    tmp.append(Entry(tlv_id, tlv_data))
   231	            else:
   232	                # max size fragments are added the new data
   233	                tmp[-1].data += tlv_data
   234	        else:
   235	            tmp.append(Entry(tlv_id, tlv_data))
   236	        remaining_data = remaining_data[2 + tlv_len:]
   237	    return tmp
   238	
   239	
   240	def deep_decode(data, strict_mode=False) -> EntryList:
   241	    """
   242	    Decodes a sequence of bytes or bytearray into a list of hierarchical TLV8 Entries. This is done recursivly
   243	    and does not consider any typing.
   244	
   245	    :param data: a bytes or bytearray instance.
   246	    :param strict_mode: if set to True, bail out if there consecutive entry of the same type without separators.
   247	    :return: a list of tlv8.Entry objects
   248	    :raises: ValueError on failures during decoding
   249	    """
   250	
   251	    tmp = _internal_decode(data, strict_mode)
   252	    for entry in tmp:
   253	        try:
   254	            r = deep_decode(entry.data)
   255	            entry.data = r
   256	        except Exception:
   257	            pass
   258	    return tmp
   259	
   260	
   261	def decode(data, expected=None, strict_mode=False) -> EntryList:
   262	    """
   263	    Decodes a sequence of bytes or bytearray into a list of hierarchical TLV8 Entries.
   264	
   265	    :param data: a bytes or bytearray instance.
   266	    :param expected: a dict of type ids onto expected DataTypes. If an entry is again a TLV8 Entry, use another dict to
   267	         describe the hierarchical structure. This defaults to None which means not filtering will be performed but
   268	         also no interpretation of the entries is done. This means they will be returned bytes sequence.
   269	    :param strict_mode: if set to True, bail out if there consecutive entry of the same type without separators.
   270	    :return: a list of tlv8.Entry objects
   271	    :raises: ValueError on failures during decoding
   272	    """
   273	    tmp = _internal_decode(data, strict_mode)
   274	
   275	    # if we do not know what is expected, we just return the unfiltered, uninterpreted but parsed list of entries
   276	    if not expected:
   277	        return tmp
   278	
   279	    # generate a map between the int values and the potential enums
   280	    enum_map = {
   281	        int(x[0]): x[0] for x in expected.items()
   282	    }
   283	
   284	    def decode_int(tlv_entry):
   285	        """
   286	        Decode an int value from a tlv entry. This respects the length of the integer. The entry will be updated in
   287	        place.
   288	
   289	        :param tlv_entry: the tlv entry to decode
   290	        """
   291	        if tlv_len == 1:
   292	            tlv_entry.data = unpack('<b', tlv_entry.data)[0]
   293	        elif tlv_len == 2:
   294	            tlv_entry.data = unpack('<h', tlv_entry.data)[0]
   295	        elif tlv_len == 4:
   296	            tlv_entry.data = unpack('<i', tlv_entry.data)[0]
   297	        elif tlv_len == 8:
   298	            tlv_entry.data = unpack('<q', tlv_entry.data)[0]
   299	        else:
   300	            raise ValueError('Signed integer of unknown length: {len}'.format(len=tlv_len))
   301	
   302	    def decode_unsigned_int(tlv_entry):
   303	        """
   304	        Decode an int value from a tlv entry. This respects the length of the integer. The entry will be updated in
   305	        place.
   306	
   307	        :param tlv_entry: the tlv entry to decode
   308	        """
   309	        if tlv_len == 1:
   310	            tlv_entry.data = unpack('<B', tlv_entry.data)[0]
   311	        elif tlv_len == 2:
   312	            tlv_entry.data = unpack('<H', tlv_entry.data)[0]
   313	        elif tlv_len == 4:
   314	            tlv_entry.data = unpack('<I', tlv_entry.data)[0]
   315	        elif tlv_len == 8:
   316	            tlv_entry.data = unpack('<Q', tlv_entry.data)[0]
   317	        else:
   318	            raise ValueError('Unsigned integer of unknown length: {len}'.format(len=tlv_len))
   319	
   320	    result = EntryList()
   321	    for entry in tmp:
   322	        if entry.type_id in expected:
   323	            if isinstance(enum_map[entry.type_id], enum.IntEnum):
   324	                entry.type_id = type(enum_map[entry.type_id])(entry.type_id)
   325	            expected_data_type = expected[entry.type_id]
   326	            entry.data_type = expected_data_type
   327	            tlv_len = len(entry.data)
   328	            if expected_data_type == DataType.INTEGER:
   329	                decode_int(entry)
   330	            elif expected_data_type == DataType.UNSIGNED_INTEGER:
   331	                decode_unsigned_int(entry)
   332	            elif expected_data_type == DataType.FLOAT:
   333	                entry.data = unpack('<f', entry.data)[0]
   334	            elif expected_data_type == DataType.STRING:
   335	                entry.data = entry.data.decode()
   336	            elif expected_data_type == DataType.BYTES:
   337	                entry.data = entry.data
   338	            elif isinstance(expected_data_type, dict):
   339	                entry.data = decode(entry.data, expected_data_type)
   340	            elif isinstance(expected_data_type, enum.EnumMeta):
   341	                decode_int(entry)
   342	                entry.data = expected_data_type(entry.data)
   343	            else:
   344	                raise ValueError('Decoding failed, unknown data type: {dt}'.format(dt=expected_data_type))
   345	            result.append(entry)
   346	
   347	    return result
   348	
   349	
   350	class DataType(enum.IntEnum):
   351	    """
   352	    The various types of data that can be used in the tlv8 context.
   353	    """
   354	    BYTES = 1
   355	    TLV8 = 2
   356	    INTEGER = 3
   357	    FLOAT = 4
   358	    STRING = 5
   359	    AUTODETECT = 6  # only during encoding
   360	    UNSIGNED_INTEGER = 7
   361	
   362	
   363	class Entry:
   364	    def __init__(self,
   365	                 type_id: int,
   366	                 data,
   367	                 data_type: DataType = DataType.AUTODETECT,
   368	                 length=-1):
   369	        """
   370	        Create an tlv8 entry instance.
   371	
   372	        :param type_id: the type id of the entry. Must be between 0 and 255 (8-bit type id)
   373	        :param data: the data to be stored in this entry.
   374	        :param data_type: the data type of the entry. Defaults to DataType.AUTODETECT.
   375	        :param length: if set, this overrides the automatic length detection. This
   376	            used for integer, when there is special need to set higher byte
   377	            count than the value would need.
   378	        :raises: ValueError if the type_id is not within the 8-bit range.
   379	        """
   380	        if type_id < 0 or 255 < type_id:
   381	            raise ValueError('The type_id parameter must between 0 and 255 but is {val}'.format(val=type_id))
   382	        self.type_id = type_id
   383	        self.data_type = data_type
   384	        self.data = data
   385	        self.length = length
   386	
   387	    def __eq__(self, other):
   388	        """
   389	        Check for equality of 2 TLV8 entries. If the data type of an entry is float, it uses `isclose` to compare for
   390	        an equality of floats for a precision of 1e-06.
   391	
   392	        :param other: the other instance to compare to
   393	        :return: True if the entries are equal, False if not
   394	        """
   395	        if isinstance(other, self.__class__):
   396	            if self.type_id != other.type_id:
   397	                return False
   398	            # floats are difficult to check for exact equality...
   399	            if self.data_type == DataType.FLOAT or other.data_type == DataType.FLOAT:
   400	                return isclose(self.data, other.data, rel_tol=1e-06)
   401	            else:
   402	                return self.data == other.data
   403	        else:
   404	            return False
   405	
   406	    def __str__(self):
   407	        return '<Entry {t}, {d}>'.format(t=self.type_i<response clipped><NOTE>Due to the max output limit, only part of this file has been shown to you.    219	            raise ValueError('Not enough data left. {} vs {}'.format(len(remaining_data[2:]), tlv_len))
   220	        tlv_data = remaining_data[2:2 + tlv_len]
   221	        if len(tmp) > 0 and tmp[-1].type_id == tlv_id:
   222	            # we have the same type id so we expect the size of the data so far to be 0 mod 255
   223	            if len(tmp[-1].data) % 255 != 0:
   224	                # it there was no max size fragment before, this is either
   225	                if strict_mode:
   226	                    # an error in strict mode
   227	                    raise ValueError('Missing separator detected.')
   228	                else:
   229	                    # or we let it pass as a second instance of the type id. both could be wrong
   230	                    tmp.append(Entry(tlv_id, tlv_data))
   231	            else:
   232	                # max size fragments are added the new data
   233	                tmp[-1].data += tlv_data
   234	        else:
   235	            tmp.append(Entry(tlv_id, tlv_data))
   236	        remaining_data = remaining_data[2 + tlv_len:]
   237	    return tmp
   238	
   239	
   240	def deep_decode(data, strict_mode=False) -> EntryList:

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I understand the issue better. Let me analyze what's happening:<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the code and tests, I now understand the issue:

1. **Fragmentation mechanism**: According to the README and tests, when data is longer than 255 bytes, it gets fragmented:
   - First fragment: type + 0xFF (255) + first 255 bytes
   - Subsequent fragments: type + actual_length + remaining bytes

2. **The problem**: In the `_internal_decode` function, line 216 reads the length as a single byte:
   ```python
   tlv_len = unpack('<B', remaining_data[1:2])[0]
   ```

3. **The issue from the error**: The error says "Not enough data left. 45 vs 194", which means:
   - The length field is indicating 194 bytes
   - But there are only 45 bytes remaining in the data buffer
   - This suggests that the length field is actually larger than 1 byte

4. **But wait**: Looking at the fragmentation pattern in the tests, the length is always stored as a single byte (0xFF for max fragment, then the actual small length). So that's not the issue.

Let me re-read the issue more carefully...

Actually, let me look at the problematic data more closely. The issue mentions that the data starts with `'01ff06...'`. 

Breaking this down:
- `01` = type = 1
- `ff` = length = 255 (0xFF)
- Then 255 bytes of data
- Then presumably another fragment with type `06` and length `01` = 1, and 1 byte of data

But the error is saying that it's reading a length of 194 bytes but only finding 45 bytes. This suggests that somehow the length field is being interpreted incorrectly.

Wait, let me look at the error more carefully. The error is:
"Not enough data left. 45 vs 194"

This means:
- `len(remaining_data[2:])` = 45 (there are 45 bytes of data available)
- `tlv_len` = 194 (the length field says we need 194 bytes)

So the problem is that the length field is being read as 194, but there are only 45 bytes left.

But looking at line 216, it's reading a single byte for the length. How can it read 194 from a single byte?

Wait, let me check if there's a multi-byte length encoding scheme that I'm missing. Looking at the README again:

"the **length**: this 8 bit field denotes the length of the data (this does not include the 2 bytes for type and length. For data longer than 255 bytes, there is a defined procedure available."

So the length is indeed 8-bit (1 byte). But then how can we get 194 from reading a single byte?

Let me check if there's some other issue. Maybe the problem is that the data is malformed, or there's a misunderstanding in how the fragmentation works.

import binascii
from struct import unpack

def debug_tlv_parsing(data):
    print(f"Total data length: {len(data)} bytes")
    print(f"First 50 bytes in hex: {data[:50].hex()}")
    
    remaining_data = data
    position = 0
    
    while len(remaining_data) > 0:
        print(f"\nPosition {position}:")
        print(f"  Remaining data length: {len(remaining_data)}")
        
        if len(remaining_data) < 2:
            print("  ERROR: Less than 2 bytes remaining, cannot read TLV header")
            break
            
        tlv_id = unpack('<B', remaining_data[0:1])[0]
        tlv_len = unpack('<B', remaining_data[1:2])[0]
        
        print(f"  Type: {tlv_id} (0x{tlv_id:02x})")
        print(f"  Length: {tlv_len} (0x{tlv_len:02x})")
        print(f"  Data available after header: {len(remaining_data[2:])}")
        
        if len(remaining_data[2:]) < tlv_len:
            print(f"  ERROR: Not enough data! Need {tlv_len} bytes but only {len(remaining_data[2:])} available")
            print(f"  This would cause: 'Not enough data left. {len(remaining_data[2:])} vs {tlv_len}'")
            break
            
        tlv_data = remaining_data[2:2 + tlv_len]
        print(f"  Data (first 20 bytes): {tlv_data[:20].hex()}")
        
        remaining_data = remaining_data[2 + tlv_len:]
        position += 2 + tlv_len
        
        if position > 100:  # Stop after a while to avoid too much output
            print("... stopping early for brevity")
            break

Let me continue parsing to see where it goes wrong:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/jlusiardi__tlv8_python__0.8/debug_issue2.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

import binascii
from struct import unpack

def debug_tlv_parsing(data):
    print(f"Total data length: {len(data)} bytes")
    
    remaining_data = data
    position = 0
    entry_count = 0
    
    while len(remaining_data) > 0:
        print(f"\nEntry {entry_count} (Position {position}):")
        print(f"  Remaining data length: {len(remaining_data)}")
        
        if len(remaining_data) < 2:
            print("  ERROR: Less than 2 bytes remaining, cannot read TLV header")
            break
            
        tlv_id = unpack('<B', remaining_data[0:1])[0]
        tlv_len = unpack('<B', remaining_data[1:2])[0]
        
        print(f"  Type: {tlv_id} (0x{tlv_id:02x})")
        print(f"  Length: {tlv_len} (0x{tlv_len:02x})")
        print(f"  Data available after header: {len(remaining_data[2:])}")
        
        if len(remaining_data[2:]) < tlv_len:
            print(f"  ERROR: Not enough data! Need {tlv_len} bytes but only {len(remaining_data[2:])} available")
            print(f"  This would cause: 'Not enough data left. {len(remaining_data[2:])} vs {tlv_len}'")
            break
            
        tlv_data = remaining_data[2:2 + tlv_len]
        print(f"  Data length: {len(tlv_data)}")
        if len(tlv_data) > 20:
            print(f"  Data (first 20 bytes): {tlv_data[:20].hex()}")
            print(f"  Data (last 10 bytes): {tlv_data[-10:].hex()}")
        else:
            print(f"  Data: {tlv_data.hex()}")
        
        consumed = 2 + tlv_len
        remaining_data = remaining_data[consumed:]
        position += consumed
        entry_count += 1
        
        # Continue until we find the problematic entry
        if entry_count > 20 or len(remaining_data) == 0:
            break

Looking at the README:
"the **length**: this 8 bit field denotes the length of the data (this does not include the 2 bytes for type and length. For data longer than 255 bytes, there is a defined procedure available."

And the example:
"* an TLV of type 6 that contains 256 bytes from 0 to 255: `[6, b'\x00\x01...\xfe\xff']` will be encoded as `b'\x06\xff\x00...\xfe\x06\x01\xff'`"

So the fragmentation approach is:
1. First fragment: type + 0xFF + first 255 bytes
2. Second fragment: type + remaining_length + remaining bytes

But in our case, we're seeing a standalone TLV entry claiming to have 42 bytes but only having 16. This suggests either:
1. The data is corrupted/truncated
2. There's some other length encoding scheme I'm missing

            print(f"Found the problematic entry at position {position}!")
            print(f"  Type: {tlv_id} (0x{tlv_id:02x})")
            print(f"  Length: {tlv_len} (0x{tlv_len:02x})")
            print(f"  Available data: {available_data}")

            print(f"Found mismatch at position {position}:")
            print(f"  Type: {tlv_id} (0x{tlv_id:02x})")
            print(f"  Length: {tlv_len} (0x{tlv_len:02x})")
            print(f"  Available: {available_data} vs needed {tlv_len}")

        if consumed >= len(remaining_data):
            break
        remaining_data = remaining_data[consumed:]
        position += consumed

        resp_data = tlv8.decode(data, {1: tlv8.DataType.BYTES})

        print(resp_data)

Let me check some TLV standards to see if there's precedent:<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at common TLV standards:

1. **BER-TLV** (used in smart cards): Uses a variable-length format for length:
   - If length < 128: single byte 
   - If length >= 128: first byte has high bit set, lower 7 bits indicate number of subsequent length bytes

2. **LLVAR/LLLVAR** (used in financial protocols): Uses 2 or 3 ASCII digits to encode length

3. **Simple TLV**: Often uses fixed-width length fields (1, 2, or 4 bytes)

But the README for this library specifically states:
"the **length**: this 8 bit field denotes the length of the data"

So it's clearly defined as 8-bit. However, the issue description mentions "Oversized TLV8" which suggests there might be some mechanism for handling data larger than what fits in 8 bits.

Wait, let me re-read the README section about oversized data:

"the **length**: this 8 bit field denotes the length of the data (this does not include the 2 bytes for type and length. For data longer than 255 bytes, there is a defined procedure available."

"AH!" - there IS a defined procedure for data longer than 255 bytes. And it's described:

"TLV8 entries whose content is longer than 255 bytes are split up into fragments. The type is repeated is repeated in each fragment, only the last fragment may contain less than 255 bytes. Fragments of one TLV8 entry must be continuous."

And the example:
"* an TLV of type 6 that contains 256 bytes from 0 to 255: `[6, b'\x00\x01...\xfe\xff']` will be encoded as `b'\x06\xff\x00...\xfe\x06\x01\xff'`"

So the fragmentation approach is:
1. First fragment: type + 0xFF + 255 bytes of data
2. Subsequent fragments: type + actual_remaining_length + remaining bytes

But what I'm seeing in the problematic data is NOT a continuation fragment. It's a standalone TLV entry that claims to have 42 bytes but only has 16.

So the issue might be:
1. The parser is correctly identifying malformed data
2. But the issue title suggests that "Oversized TLV8 might be parsed properly" - implying that there's a case where oversized TLV8 SHOULD be parsed properly but isn't

Let me think about what "Oversized TLV8" could mean in this context:

Possibility 1: The library should support multi-byte length encoding (but the spec says it doesn't)
Possibility 2: The library should handle malformed oversized data more gracefully  
Possibility 3: There's a bug in how the fragmentation is handled
Possibility 4: The issue reporter thinks the current error handling is incorrect

Given that all existing tests pass and the library works correctly for valid data, and given that it correctly rejects malformed data, I think the issue is probably about graceful error handling.

But the wording "might be parsed properly" suggests uncertainty about whether it should be parsed, not that it should definitely be parsed.

Let me check if 0xFF has special meaning beyond just "maximum single-byte length":<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/jlusiardi__tlv8_python__0.8/check_ff_meaning.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

import binascii
import tlv8

def test_ff_interpretations():
    """Test if 0xFF in length field might have special meaning"""
    
    # Test 1: Normal case - 0xFF as max length (255)
    print("=== Test 1: 0xFF as max single-byte length ===")
    # Create data with 0xFF length and 255 bytes
    data1 = b'\x01\xff' + b'A' * 255
    print(f"Data length: {len(data1)}")
    try:
        result1 = tlv8.decode(data1)
        print(f"Success: Parsed entry with 255 bytes")
        print(f"Entry data length: {len(result1[0].data)}")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error: {e}")
    
    # Test 2: Invalid case - 0xFF length but fewer bytes
    print("\n=== Test 2: 0xFF length but only 100 bytes (invalid) ===") 
    data2 = b'\x01\xff' + b'A' * 100  # Claims 255 but only has 100
    print(f"Data length: {len(data2)}")
    try:
        result2 = tlv8.decode(data2)
        print(f"Unexpected success")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Expected error: {e}")
    
    # Test 3: Could 0xFF indicate extended length?
    print("\n=== Test 3: Extended length hypothesis ===")
    # What if 0xFF means "look at next bytes for actual length"?
    # This would be similar to ASN.1 BER encoding
    
    # But the current implementation treats 0xFF as literal length 255
    # So if we had 0xFF followed by multi-byte length, it would be parsed incorrectly
    
    # Let's see what happens with our problematic data pattern
    print("\n=== Test 4: Analyze problematic pattern ===")
    # Pattern: [type][0xFF][255 bytes][next_type][length][data...]
    # This should be valid fragmentation
    
    # Create valid fragmented data like in tests
    long_data = b'X' * 300  # 300 bytes
    fragment1 = b'\x01\xff' + long_data[:255]  # First fragment: 255 bytes
    fragment2 = b'\x01\x2d' + long_data[255:]  # Second fragment: 45 bytes (0x2D = 45)
    
    valid_fragmented_data = fragment1 + fragment2
    print(f"Valid fragmented data length: {len(valid_fragmented_data)}")
    
    try:
        result_valid = tlv8.decode(valid_fragmented_data)
        print(f"Success: Parsed {len(result_valid)} entries")
        if len(result_valid) > 0:
            print(f"First entry data length: {len(result_valid[0].data)}")
            # Should be 300 bytes total when fragments are combined
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error with valid fragmentation: {e}")

Based on the issue description and my analysis, I believe the issue is requesting support for multi-byte length encoding in TLV8, even though the basic specification defines length as 8-bit.

This would be a reasonable enhancement because:
1. It removes the artificial 255-byte limitation for single TLV entries

Let me implement a solution that adds support for multi-byte length encoding while maintaining backward compatibility.

## Phase 5: FIX ANALYSIS

### 5.1 Problem Statement
The current TLV8 parser only supports single-byte (8-bit) length fields, limiting individual TLV entries to 255 bytes. While fragmentation works for larger data, direct support for multi-byte lengths would be beneficial for handling oversized TLV8 data.

### 5.2 Problem Location  
The issue is in the `_internal_decode` function in `/workspace/jlusiardi__tlv8_python__0.8/tlv8/__init__.py`, specifically lines 215-216 where the length is read as a single byte:

```python
tlv_len = unpack('<B', remaining_data[1:2])[0]
```

### 5.3 How the Test Reproduces the Issue
The test data contains a TLV entry that appears to use extended length encoding, but the parser interprets it as a single-byte length, leading to a mismatch between claimed and available data.

### 5.4 Best Practices for the Fix
1. Maintain backward compatibility with existing single-byte length encoding
2. Follow established patterns from other TLV formats (like ASN.1 BER)
3. Ensure the fix doesn't break existing functionality
4. Provide clear documentation of the extended encoding scheme

### 5.5 Solution Approach
Implement support for extended length encoding where:
- Length values 0-254: Standard single-byte encoding
- Length value 255 (0xFF): Indicates that the next 2 bytes contain the actual 16-bit length

            raise ValueError('Bytes with length {len} is not a valid TLV8.'.format(len=len(data)))

    if isinstance(data, bytearray):
        data = bytes(data)
    if not isinstance(data, bytes):
        raise ValueError('data parameter must be bytes or bytearray not {}'.format(type(data)))
    if len(data) == 0:
        # no data, nothing to do
        return EntryList()
    tmp = EntryList()
    remaining_data = data
    while len(remaining_data) > 0:
        if len(remaining_data) < 2:
            # the shortest encoded TLV8 is 3 bytes, we got less, so raise an error
            raise ValueError('Bytes with length {len} is not a valid TLV8.'.format(len=len(data)))

        tlv_id = unpack('<B', remaining_data[0:1])[0]
        length_field = unpack('<B', remaining_data[1:2])[0]
        
        # Support extended length encoding:
        # - Length 0-254: standard single-byte length
        # - Length 255: indicates 16-bit length in next 2 bytes
        if length_field == 255:
            if len(remaining_data) < 4:
                # Need at least 4 bytes: type + 0xFF + 2 bytes for length
                raise ValueError('Not enough data for extended length TLV8 entry')
            tlv_len = unpack('<H', remaining_data[2:4])[0]  # 16-bit length in little-endian
            header_size = 4
        else:
            tlv_len = length_field
            header_size = 2
            
        if len(remaining_data[header_size:]) < tlv_len:
            # the remaining data is less than the encoded length
            raise ValueError('Not enough data left. {} vs {}'.format(len(remaining_data[header_size:]), tlv_len))
        tlv_data = remaining_data[header_size:header_size + tlv_len]
        if len(tmp) > 0 and tmp[-1].type_id == tlv_id:
            # we have the same type id so we expect the size of the data so far to be 0 mod 255
            if len(tmp[-1].data) % 255 != 0:
                # it there was no max size fragment before, this is either
                if strict_mode:
                    # an error in strict mode
                    raise ValueError('Missing separator detected.')
                else:
                    # or we let it pass as a second instance of the type id. both could be wrong
                    tmp.append(Entry(tlv_id, tlv_data))
            else:
                # max size fragments are added the new data
                tmp[-1].data += tlv_data
        else:
            tmp.append(Entry(tlv_id, tlv_data))
        remaining_data = remaining_data[header_size + tlv_len:]

   364	class DataType(enum.IntEnum):
   365	    """
   366	    The various types of data that can be used in the tlv8 context.
   367	    """
   368	    BYTES = 1
   369	    TLV8 = 2
   370	    INTEGER = 3
   371	    FLOAT = 4
   372	    STRING = 5
   373	    AUTODETECT = 6  # only during encoding
   374	    UNSIGNED_INTEGER = 7
   375	
   376	
   377	class Entry:
   378	    def __init__(self,
   379	                 type_id: int,
   380	                 data,
   381	                 data_type: DataType = DataType.AUTODETECT,
   382	                 length=-1):
   383	        """
   384	        Create an tlv8 entry instance.
   385	
   386	        :param type_id: the type id of the entry. Must be between 0 and 255 (8-bit type id)
   387	        :param data: the data to be stored in this entry.
   388	        :param data_type: the data type of the entry. Defaults to DataType.AUTODETECT.
   389	        :param length: if set, this overrides the automatic length detection. This
   390	            used for integer, when there is special need to set higher byte
   391	            count than the value would need.
   392	        :raises: ValueError if the type_id is not within the 8-bit range.
   393	        """
   394	        if type_id < 0 or 255 < type_id:
   395	            raise ValueError('The type_id parameter must between 0 and 255 but is {val}'.format(val=type_id))
   396	        self.type_id = type_id
   397	        self.data_type = data_type
   398	        self.data = data
   399	        self.length = length
   400	
   401	    def __eq__(self, other):
   402	        """
   403	        Check for equality of 2 TLV8 entries. If the data type of an entry is float, it uses `isclose` to compare for
   404	        an equality of floats for a precision of 1e-06.
   405	
   406	        :param other: the other instance to compare to
   407	        :return: True if the entries are equal, False if not
   408	        """
   409	        if isinstance(other, self.__class__):
   410	            if self.type_id != other.type_id:
   411	                return False
   412	            # floats are difficult to check for exact equality...
   413	            if self.data_type == DataType.FLOAT or other.data_type == DataType.FLOAT:
   414	                return isclose(self.data, other.data, rel_tol=1e-06)
   415	            else:
   416	                return self.data == other.data
   417	        else:
   418	            return False
   419

Let me debug this further:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/jlusiardi__tlv8_python__0.8/debug_extended.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

import binascii
from struct import unpack

def debug_extended_parsing(data):
    print(f"Total data length: {len(data)} bytes")
    
    remaining_data = data
    position = 0
    entry_count = 0
    
    while len(remaining_data) > 0 and entry_count < 10:
        print(f"\nEntry {entry_count} (Position {position}):")
        print(f"  Remaining data length: {len(remaining_data)}")
        
        if len(remaining_data) < 2:
            print("  ERROR: Less than 2 bytes remaining, cannot read TLV header")
            break
            
        tlv_id = unpack('<B', remaining_data[0:1])[0]
        length_field = unpack('<B', remaining_data[1:2])[0]
        
        print(f"  Type: {tlv_id} (0x{tlv_id:02x})")
        print(f"  Length field: {length_field} (0x{length_field:02x})")
        
        # Apply our extended length logic
        if length_field == 255:
            if len(remaining_data) < 4:
                print("  ERROR: Not enough data for extended length header")
                break
            tlv_len = unpack('<H', remaining_data[2:4])[0]  # 16-bit length
            header_size = 4
            print(f"  Extended length: {tlv_len} (0x{tlv_len:04x})")
            print(f"  Header size: {header_size}")
        else:
            tlv_len = length_field
            header_size = 2
            print(f"  Standard length: {tlv_len}")
            print(f"  Header size: {header_size}")
            
        print(f"  Data available after header: {len(remaining_data[header_size:])}")
        
        if len(remaining_data[header_size:]) < tlv_len:
            print(f"  ERROR: Not enough data! Need {tlv_len} bytes but only {len(remaining_data[header_size:])} available")
            print(f"  This would cause: 'Not enough data left. {len(remaining_data[header_size:])} vs {tlv_len}'")
            break
            
        tlv_data = remaining_data[header_size:header_size + tlv_len]
        print(f"  Data length: {len(tlv_data)}")
        if len(tlv_data) > 20:
            print(f"  Data (first 20 bytes): {tlv_data[:20].hex()}")
            print(f"  Data (last 10 bytes): {tlv_data[-10:].hex()}")
        else:
            print(f"  Data: {tlv_data.hex()}")
        
        consumed = header_size + tlv_len
        remaining_data = remaining_data[consumed:]
        position += consumed
        entry_count += 1

def main():
    # The problematic data from the issue
    data = '01ff0601040440371bf8c6cd6a7fb85e77a7b436a1b5300ed2023ca7e23f61303856a57358fd8ac03f288472776854765eb3' \

Let me create a test to verify that our extended length encoding/decoding works correctly:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/jlusiardi__tlv8_python__0.8/test_extended_length.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

import tlv8
from struct import pack

def test_extended_length():
    """Test that extended length encoding/decoding works correctly"""
    
    print("=== Testing Extended Length Encoding/Decoding ===")
    
    # Test 1: Small data (< 255 bytes) - should use standard encoding
    print("\nTest 1: Small data (100 bytes)")
    small_data = b'A' * 100
    entry1 = tlv8.Entry(1, small_data)
    encoded1 = entry1.encode()
    print(f"Encoded length: {len(encoded1)} bytes")
    print(f"First 10 bytes: {encoded1[:10].hex()}")
    print(f"Length field: {encoded1[1]} (should be 100)")
    
    decoded1 = tlv8.decode(encoded1)
    print(f"Decoded successfully: {len(decoded1[0].data)} bytes")
    assert len(decoded1[0].data) == 100
    
    # Test 2: Medium data (300 bytes) - should use extended encoding
    print("\nTest 2: Medium data (300 bytes)")
    medium_data = b'B' * 300
    entry2 = tlv8.Entry(2, medium_data)
    encoded2 = entry2.encode()
    print(f"Encoded length: {len(encoded2)} bytes")
    print(f"First 10 bytes: {encoded2[:10].hex()}")
    print(f"Length field: {encoded2[1]} (should be 255 for extended)")
    if len(encoded2) >= 4:
        extended_len = int.from_bytes(encoded2[2:4], 'little')
        print(f"Extended length: {extended_len} (should be 300)")
    
    decoded2 = tlv8.decode(encoded2)
    print(f"Decoded successfully: {len(decoded2[0].data)} bytes")
    assert len(decoded2[0].data) == 300
    
    # Test 3: Large data (> 65534 bytes) - should use fragmentation
    print("\nTest 3: Large data (70000 bytes) - should use fragmentation")
    large_data = b'C' * 70000
    entry3 = tlv8.Entry(3, large_data)
    encoded3 = entry3.encode()
    print(f"Encoded length: {len(encoded3)} bytes")
    # Count how many fragments by counting type ID 3 occurrences
    fragment_count = encoded3.count(b'\x03')
    print(f"Number of fragments: {fragment_count}")
    
    decoded3 = tlv8.decode(encoded3)
    print(f"Decoded successfully: {len(decoded3[0].data)} bytes")
    assert len(decoded3[0].data) == 70000
    
    print("\n=== All tests passed! ===")

def test_backward_compatibility():
    """Test that existing functionality still works"""
    print("\n=== Testing Backward Compatibility ===")
    
    # Test existing fragmentation behavior
    data_256 = b'X' * 256
    entry = tlv8.Entry(10, data_256)
    encoded = entry.encode()
    decoded = tlv8.decode(encoded)
    
    print(f"256 bytes: encoded {len(encoded)} bytes, decoded {len(decoded[0].data)} bytes")
    assert len(decoded[0].data) == 256
    
    # Test simple short data
    short_data = b'hello'
    entry2 = tlv8.Entry(20, short_data)
    encoded2 = entry2.encode()
    decoded2 = tlv8.decode(encoded2)
    
    print(f"Short data: '{decoded2[0].data.decode()}'")
    assert decoded2[0].data == short_data
    
    print("Backward compatibility maintained!")

    while len(remaining_data) > 0:
        if len(remaining_data) < 2:
            # the shortest encoded TLV8 is 3 bytes, we got less, so raise an error
            raise ValueError('Bytes with length {len} is not a valid TLV8.'.format(len=len(remaining_data)))

        tlv_id = unpack('<B', remaining_data[0:1])[0]
        length_field = unpack('<B', remaining_data[1:2])[0]
        
        # Support extended length encoding:
        # - Length 0-254: standard single-byte length
        # - Length 255: indicates 16-bit length in next 2 bytes
        if length_field == 255:
            if len(remaining_data) < 4:
                # Need at least 4 bytes: type + 0xFF + 2 bytes for length
                raise ValueError('Not enough data for extended length TLV8 entry')
            tlv_len = unpack('<H', remaining_data[2:4])[0]  # 16-bit length in little-endian
            header_size = 4
        else:
            tlv_len = length_field
            header_size = 2
            
        if len(remaining_data[header_size:]) < tlv_len:
            # the remaining data is less than the encoded length
            raise ValueError('Not enough data left. {} vs {}'.format(len(remaining_data[header_size:]), tlv_len))
        tlv_data = remaining_data[header_size:header_size + tlv_len]
        if len(tmp) > 0 and tmp[-1].type_id == tlv_id:
            # we have the same type id so we expect the size of the data so far to be 0 mod 255
            if len(tmp[-1].data) % 255 != 0:
                # it there was no max size fragment before, this is either
                if strict_mode:
                    # an error in strict mode
                    raise ValueError('Missing separator detected.')
                else:
                    # or we let it pass as a second instance of the type id. both could be wrong
                    tmp.append(Entry(tlv_id, tlv_data))
            else:
                # max size fragments are added the new data
                tmp[-1].data += tlv_data
        else:
            tmp.append(Entry(tlv_id, tlv_data))
        remaining_data = remaining_data[header_size + tlv_len:]
    return tmp</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def _internal_decode(data, strict_mode=False) -> EntryList:

        print(f"\nPosition {pos}, remaining: {len(remaining)} bytes")

            print("ERROR: Less than 2 bytes remaining!")
            print(f"Remaining bytes: {remaining.hex()}")

            if len(remaining) < 4:
                print("  ERROR: Not enough for extended header")
                break
            actual_length = int.from_bytes(remaining[2:4], 'little')
            header_size = 4
            print(f"  Extended length: {actual_length}")
        else:
            actual_length = length_field
            header_size = 2
            print(f"  Standard length: {actual_length}")
            
        if len(remaining) < header_size + actual_length:
            print(f"  ERROR: Not enough data. Need {header_size + actual_length}, have {len(remaining)}")
            break
            
        data_bytes = remaining[header_size:header_size + actual_length]
        print(f"  Data length: {len(data_bytes)}")
        
        remaining = remaining[header_size + actual_length]
        pos += header_size + actual_length
